📘 How to Use This Material

  1. Read the Quick Shortcuts box before starting a topic — use it in the exam, not long-form calculation.
  2. Click a question to reveal the step-by-step solution — try solving it yourself first.
  3. Wrong answers go into a separate mistake notebook — revisit them weekly.
  4. This document prints cleanly — open it and use your browser's Print to get a PDF with all answers visible.
What's new in this edition: Added 6 topics that were missing from the SSC CGL Quant syllabus — Partnership, Boats & Streams, Problems on Ages, Surds & Indices, Simple & Quadratic Equations, and Advanced Mensuration (Trapezium/Rhombus/Sector/Parallelogram). Also added missing formulas to existing topics: population growth/decline (Percentage), false-weight profit trick (Profit & Loss), and circle theorems (Geometry). This now covers the full Tier 1 Quant syllabus.

TOPIC 1

Number System

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Unit digit cycles: 2→(2,4,8,6) 3→(3,9,7,1) 4→(4,6) 7→(7,9,3,1) 8→(8,4,2,6) 9→(9,1); 0,1,5,6 repeat themselves
  • Divisibility by 11: (sum of odd-place digits) − (sum of even-place digits) = 0 or a multiple of 11
  • Number of factors of a^p × b^q = (p+1)(q+1)
  • Sum of first n natural numbers = n(n+1)/2
Q1Find the unit digit of 8^70.
  1. Cyclicity of 8 is [8, 4, 2, 6] (length 4).
  2. 70 mod 4 = 2, so use position 2 in the cycle.
Answer: 4
Q2Find the unit digit of 8^112.
  1. Cyclicity of 8 is [8, 4, 2, 6] (length 4).
  2. 112 mod 4 = 0, so use position 4 in the cycle.
Answer: 6
Q3Find the unit digit of 7^33.
  1. Cyclicity of 7 is [7, 9, 3, 1] (length 4).
  2. 33 mod 4 = 1, so use position 1 in the cycle.
Answer: 7

Q41Is 9166 divisible by 11?
  1. Sum of digits at odd places (from right) = 7
  2. Sum of digits at even places = 15
  3. Difference = -8
Answer: No, not divisible by 11

Q91Is 241 a prime number?
  1. √241 ≈ 15.5 — check divisibility by primes up to that value.
  2. Test 2,3,5,7,11,13... up to that limit.
Answer: Yes, prime

  2. Test 2,3,5,7,11,13... up to that limit.

TOPIC 2

HCF & LCM

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • HCF × LCM = product of the two numbers (only for 2 numbers)
  • Largest number dividing x,y leaving remainders a,b → HCF(x−a, y−b)
  • Smallest number divisible by x,y,z leaving remainder r each time → LCM(x,y,z) + r
Q1Find the HCF and LCM of 30 and 90.
  1. HCF(30,90) by Euclid's method = 30
  2. LCM = (30×90)/HCF = 2700/30
Answer: HCF=30, LCM=90
Q2Find the HCF and LCM of 57 and 33.
  1. HCF(57,33) by Euclid's method = 3
  2. LCM = (57×33)/HCF = 1881/3
Answer: HCF=3, LCM=627
Q3Find the HCF and LCM of 82 and 89.
  1. HCF(82,89) by Euclid's method = 1
  2. LCM = (82×89)/HCF = 7298/1
Answer: HCF=1, LCM=7298
Q4Find the HCF and LCM of 35 and 61.
  1. HCF(35,61) by Euclid's method = 1
  2. LCM = (35×61)/HCF = 2135/1
Answer: HCF=1, LCM=2135
Q5Find the HCF and LCM of 49 and 66.

Answer: HCF=1, LCM=3234

TOPIC 4

Percentage

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • x% of y = y% of x
  • If A is x% more than B → B is less than A by [x/(100+x)]×100%
  • Successive % change a,b → net = a + b + (ab/100)
  • Original value after p% increase to N: original = N×100/(100+p)
  • Population after n years of r% growth: P(1+r/100)^n | of r% decline: P(1−r/100)^n

TOPIC 5

Ratio & Proportion

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • a:b = c:d ⇒ a×d = b×c (cross multiply)
  • Dividing total T in ratio a:b:c → parts = T×a/(a+b+c), etc.
  • Mean proportional between a,b = √(ab)
Q1Divide 50 between A and B in the ratio 7:3.
  1. Total parts = 7+3 = 10
  2. A's share = 50 × 7/10
  3. B's share = 50 − A's share
Answer: A=35, B=15

TOPIC 6

Average

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Average = Sum of observations / Number of observations
  • Average of first n naturals=(n+1)/2; first n even=n+1; first n odd=n
  • New sum − old sum = n × (change in average)

TOPIC 7

Profit, Loss & Discount

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Profit% = (SP−CP)/CP × 100 ; Loss% = (CP−SP)/CP × 100
  • SP = CP × (100±%)/100
  • Successive discounts a%,b% → net = a+b−ab/100
  • MP relation: SP = MP × (100−Discount%)/100
  • False weight trick: Gain% = (True weight − False weight)/False weight × 100

TOPIC 8

Simple & Compound Interest

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • SI = (P × R × T)/100
  • CI = P[(1+R/100)^T − 1]
  • CI − SI for 2 years = P(R/100)²
  • Half-yearly: use R/2 and 2T

TOPIC 9

Time and Work

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Work rate = 1/(days to finish); combined rate = sum of individual rates
  • Efficiency is inversely proportional to time taken
  • Man-days rule: M1×D1 = M2×D2
  • Pipes: inlet = +rate, outlet/leak = −rate
Q1A can finish a work in 21 days, B in 20 days. In how many days will they finish it together?
  1. A's 1-day work = 1/21; B's 1-day work = 1/20
  2. Combined 1-day work = 1/21 + 1/20
  3. Total days = 1 / (combined rate)
Answer: 10.24 days

  3. Total days = 1 / (combined rate)

TOPIC 10

Time, Speed & Distance

⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Speed=Distance/Time; km/hr→m/s ×5/18; m/s→km/hr ×18/5
  • Average speed (equal distances a,b) = 2ab/(a+b)
  • Relative speed: same direction → difference; opposite → sum
  • Train crossing time = (sum of lengths)/(relative speed)

  2. = 400 / 5

TOPIC 11

Mensuration

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Rectangle: Area=l×b, Perimeter=2(l+b) | Square: Area=a², Diagonal=a√2
  • Circle: Area=πr², Circumference=2πr
  • Cube: Vol=a³, SA=6a² | Cuboid: Vol=lbh | Cylinder: Vol=πr²h | Cone: Vol=⅓πr²h | Sphere: Vol=4/3πr³
Q1Find the area and perimeter of a rectangle with length 21 m and breadth 15 m.
  1. Area = l×b = 21×15
  2. Perimeter = 2(l+b) = 2×(21+15)
Answer: Area=315 m², Perimeter=72 m

  • (a+b)²=a²+2ab+b² ; (a−b)²=a²−2ab+b² ; a²−b²=(a+b)(a−b)
  • x+1/x=k → x²+1/x²=k²−2 ; x³+1/x³=k³−3k
  • x−1/x=k → x²+1/x²=k²+2 ; x³−1/x³=k³+3k
  • If a+b+c=0 → a³+b³+c³=3abc

⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • sin²θ+cos²θ=1 | 1+tan²θ=sec²θ
  • Pythagoras: hyp² = base² + height²
  • Angle in a semicircle = 90° | Triangle angle sum = 180°
  • Standard values: sin30=1/2, cos30=√3/2, tan45=1, sin60=√3/2, cos60=1/2
  • Circle: tangent ⊥ radius at point of contact | tangents from an external point are equal in length
  • Angles in the same segment of a circle are equal | Opposite angles of a cyclic quadrilateral sum to 180°

TOPIC 14

Mixture & Alligation

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Alligation: (cheaper qty)/(dearer qty) = (dearer price − mean)/(mean − cheaper price)
  • Repeated dilution: final = initial × (1 − removed/total)^n
Q1In what ratio should a cheaper item worth ₹30/kg be mixed with a dearer item worth ₹110/kg to get a mixture worth ₹43/kg?
  1. Ratio = (dearer−mean) : (mean−cheaper)
  2. = (110−43) : (43−30) = 67:13
Answer: 67:13
Q2In what ratio should a cheaper item worth ₹36/kg be mixed with a dearer item worth ₹75/kg to get a mixture worth ₹40/kg?

  2. = (75−40) : (40−36) = 35:4
Answer: 35:4

TOPIC 15

Data Interpretation

⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Read the title, units, and legend FIRST before touching the questions
  • Pie chart: 360° = 100%, so degrees ÷ 3.6 = percentage
  • For % change, approximate instead of exact division when options are far apart
  • Ratio/average DI questions often let you cancel common factors

  2. % change = (32/214) × 100
Answer: 14.95% increase
Q116[Data Set 10] Find the % change in sales of Store D from 2024 to 2025.
  1. Change = 316−114 = 202
  2. % change = (202/114) × 100
Answer: 177.19% increase
Q117[Data Set 10] Find the % change in sales of Store E from 2024 to 2025.
  1. Change = 165−237 = -72
  2. % change = (-72/237) × 100
Answer: 30.38% decrease
Q118[Data Set 10] Find the average sales per store in 2024.
  1. Average = Total / Count = 886/5
Answer: 177.2
Q119[Data Set 10] Find the ratio of total sales in 2024 to 2025.
  1. Ratio = 886:1204
  2. Simplify by HCF (2)
Answer: 443:602
Q120[Data Set 10] Find the difference between 2025 sales of Store C and Store D.
  1. 246 − 316
Answer: -70

TOPIC 16

Partnership

80 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Simple partnership (same time invested): profit divided in ratio of capitals
  • Compound partnership (different capital AND time): profit ratio = (capital × time) for each partner
  • If a partner leaves/joins midway, calculate capital×months separately for each period
Q1A and B invest ₹7728 and ₹7107 respectively in a business for the same time. If total profit is ₹8000, find each share.
  1. Profit ratio = capital ratio = 7728:7107 = 112:103
  2. A's share = 8000 × 112/215
  3. B's share = 8000 × 103/215
Answer: A=₹4167.44, B=₹3832.56

TOPIC 17

Boats & Streams

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Downstream speed = boat speed + stream speed (u+v)
  • Upstream speed = boat speed − stream speed (u−v)
  • Boat speed = ½(downstream+upstream); Stream speed = ½(downstream−upstream)
  • Time = Distance/Speed — use downstream speed going with current, upstream against it
Q1A boat's speed in still water is 11 km/hr and the stream's speed is 3 km/hr. Find the downstream and upstream speeds.
  1. Downstream = 11+3
  2. Upstream = 11−3
Answer: Downstream=14 km/hr, Upstream=8 km/hr
Q2A boat's speed in still water is 10 km/hr and the stream's speed is 3 km/hr. Find the downstream and upstream speeds.
  1. Downstream = 10+3
  2. Upstream = 10−3
Answer: Downstream=13 km/hr, Upstream=7 km/hr

  2. Time = Distance/Speed = 79/22
Answer: 3.59 hours

TOPIC 18

Problems on Ages

85 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Represent unknown present ages as variables (e.g., x, x+diff) and form an equation from the given time condition
  • 'n years ago' → subtract n from present age; 'after n years' → add n
  • If ratio of ages is a:b now, use ages = ak, bk and solve using the future/past condition
Q1A father is 24 years older than his son. After 12 years, the son's age will be 24. Find the present ages of both.
  1. Son's present age = 24 − 12 = 12
  2. Father's present age = 12 + 24 = 36
Answer: Son=12 yrs, Father=36 yrs
Q2A father is 5 years older than his son. After 13 years, the son's age will be 24. Find the present ages of both.
  1. Son's present age = 24 − 13 = 11
  2. Father's present age = 11 + 5 = 16
Answer: Son=11 yrs, Father=16 yrs
Q3A father is 2 years older than his son. After 6 years, the son's age will be 15. Find the present ages of both.
  1. Son's present age = 15 − 6 = 9
  2. Father's present age = 9 + 2 = 11
Answer: Son=9 yrs, Father=11 yrs
Q4A father is 21 years older than his son. After 9 years, the son's age will be 19. Find the present ages of both.
  1. Son's present age = 19 − 9 = 10
  2. Father's present age = 10 + 21 = 31
Answer: Son=10 yrs, Father=31 yrs

Q31The ratio of A's and B's present ages is 5:7. If A is 40 years old, find B's age, and both their ages after 9 years.
  1. Let common multiple = k. 5k = 40 → k = 8
  2. B's age = 7×8 = 56
  3. After 9 years: A=40+9, B=56+9
Answer: B now=56, A after=49, B after=65

TOPIC 19

Surds & Indices

90 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • a^m × a^n = a^(m+n) | a^m ÷ a^n = a^(m−n) | (a^m)^n = a^(mn)
  • a^0 = 1 (a≠0) | a^(−m) = 1/a^m | a^(1/n) = ⁿ√a
  • √a × √b = √(ab) | To rationalize 1/√a, multiply top & bottom by √a

TOPIC 20

Simple & Quadratic Equations

85 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Linear equation ax+b=c → x = (c−b)/a
  • Quadratic formula: x = [−b ± √(b²−4ac)] / 2a for ax²+bx+c=0
  • Sum of roots = −b/a ; Product of roots = c/a
  • Factorization method is faster than the formula when roots are whole numbers

  2. x = 90/10

  1. Sum of roots = −b/a = 11
  2. Product of roots = c/a = 24
  3. Factorize to find two numbers with this sum and product
Answer: x = 3 or x = 8

Q66If the roots of a quadratic equation are -10 and 6, find the sum and product of roots, and form the equation.
  1. Sum = -10+6 = -4
  2. Product = -10×6 = -60
  3. Equation: x² − (sum)x + (product) = 0
Answer: x² − -4x + -60 = 0
Q67If the roots of a quadratic equation are 0 and -3, find the sum and product of roots, and form the equation.

Answer: x² − -3x + 0 = 0

TOPIC 21

Advanced Mensuration (Trapezium, Rhombus, Sector)

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Trapezium: Area = ½ × (sum of parallel sides) × height
  • Rhombus: Area = ½ × d1 × d2 (diagonals) | Parallelogram: Area = base × height
  • Sector of circle: Area = (θ/360)×πr² ; Arc length = (θ/360)×2πr
  • Regular hexagon: Area = (3√3/2)a²

  1. Area = ½×(sum of parallel sides)×height
  2. = ½×(10+14)×16

  2. Arc length = (θ/360)×2πr
Answer: Area=154.0 cm², Arc=22.0 cm
